0

\log 2

\log 2

\log m

0

Table 2:
Relative entropies when
u\sim v

8
Concluding remarks: open questions and perspective

In this concluding section, we briefly summarize our findings and discuss possible future directions. Consider a pair of subfactors of type
II_{1}
factors :

\begin{matrix}P&\subset&M\cr&&\cup\cr&&Q\end{matrix}

with both
[M:P]\,,\,[M:Q]<\infty
. This work is guided by our attempt to answer the following question :

Open Problem 1:
Given a pair of subfactors
P,Q\subset M
, provide formulae to compute the invariants
\lambda(P,Q),\,\mbox{Ang}_{M}(P,Q)
, and
H(P|Q)
.

This appears to be a difficult problem. There are two major obstacles in attacking this problem :

(i)

It may very well happen that
P\cap Q
is not a factor.

(ii)

The index of
[M:P\cap Q]
may be infinite.

To attack problem
1
stated above, it seems reasonable to answer first the following particular case.

Open Problem 2:
Consider a quadruple of
II_{1}
factors
(N\subset P,Q\subset M)
with
[M:N]<\infty
. How to compute the invariants
\,\lambda(P,Q),\,\mbox{Ang}_{M}(P,Q),\,\alpha^{N}_{M}(P,Q)
,
\beta^{N}_{M}(P,Q),\,H(P|Q)?

One may expect that
H
is related to
\lambda
(for example,
7.52
). Only the formula for
\lambda(P,Q)
in this irreducible situation is known due to
[
4
]
. Even if
P\cap Q
is a factor with
[M:P\cap Q]<\infty
, and
P\subset M,\,Q\subset M
both are irreducible, then also
P\cap Q\subset M
may not be irreducible (see
6.18
, for instance). In view of the fact that
\lambda
and
H
are well-behaved under ‘controlled’ limit of finite dimensional grid, it seems important to know the value of them in finite dimension. However, we stumbled upon the following problem.

Open problem 3:
Given two finite-dimensional von Neumann-subalgebras
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{C}
of a finite-dimensional von Neumann algebra
\mathcal{A}
, can we obtain formulae for
\lambda(\mathcal{B,C})
and
H(\mathcal{B}|\mathcal{C})
similar to Section 6 of
[
36
]
?

The following simpler version of this problem is open.

Open Problem 4:
(M. Choda and D. Petz) What is the formula for
H(\Delta_{n}|u\Delta_{n}u^{*})
, where
u\in M_{n}(\mathbb{C})
is a unitary matrix?

To tackle Problem 1, it is evident that we need to first compute the invariants for important cases in order to gain insight. Motivated by this goal, in this article we have considered a pair
(u,v)
of distinct complex Hadamard matrices, and attempted to compute these invariants for the corresponding pair of spin model subfactors
R_{u},R_{v}\subset R
. A complete characterization of
R_{u}\neq R_{v}
has been obtained. Given distinct
2\times 2
complex Hadamard matrices
u
and
v
, we have obtained a quadruple of
II_{1}
factors which has been completely characterized, and the invariants are computed. The intersection of the corresponding spin model subfactors turns out to be a vertex model subfactor of index
4
. The relative position between the spin model subfactors in the
2\times 2
situation is completely understood. However, the Hadamard inequivalent
4\times 4
case is more involved and quite surprising. Below we briefly explain the
4\times 4
case in a more precise manner.

Consider a pair
(u(a),u(b))
of
4\times 4
Hadamard inequivalent distinct complex Hadamard matrices parametrized by the circle parameters
a,b\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
(semicircle to be precise). Note that if
a=e^{i\phi}
and
b=e^{i\psi}
, then
\phi,\psi\in[0,\pi)
(so that
\phi-\psi\in(-\pi,\pi)
). We obtain a pair of spin model subfactors
R_{u(a)}\subset R
and
R_{u(b)}\subset R
. Let
\alpha
be the angle between the two circle parameters
a
and
b
as described in
Figure

17
. Then, the following two cases arise.

Figure 17:
Angle between circle parameters

Case I:

\alpha=2k\pi/m
for some
m\in 2\mathbb{N}+2
and
1\leq k\leq m-1
with
k\neq m/2
. In this case, we have
R_{u(a)}\cap R_{u(b)}
is a factor and
[R:R_{u(a)}\cap R_{u(b)}]=2m
. Also,
H(R_{u(a)}|R_{u(b)})=H(R_{u(b)}|R_{u(a)})=\log 2
.

In this case, there are two major ingredients. The first one is proving that the intersection of
R_{u(a)}\cap R_{u(b)}
is a factor, for which a bit of work is needed. We remark that we thereby obtain a series of potentially new finite index and irreducible subfactors of
R
. The second ingredient is proving that
R_{u(a)}\vee R_{u(b)}
is a subfactor of
R
with index
2
.

Open problem 5:
What is the principal graph of the family of subfactors
R_{u(a)}\cap R_{u(b)}\subset R
? Can we describe the planar algebra?

Case II:

\alpha\neq 2k\pi/m
for any
m\in 2\mathbb{N}+2
with
1\leq k\leq m-1
. In this case
R_{u(a)}\cap R_{u(b)}
has infinite Pimsner-Popa index in
R
.

Open Problem 6:
For Case II, the following problems seem to be of interest.

(i)

Is it true that
\,R_{u(a)}\cap R_{u(b)}
is a factor?

(ii)

What is the relative commutant
{(R_{u(a)}\cap R_{u(b)})}^{\prime}\cap R\,
?

(iii)

How to compute
\text{Ang}_{R}(R_{u(a)},R_{u(b)})
?

(iv)

What are the values of relative entropies
H(R_{u(a)}|R_{u(b)})
and
H(R|R_{u(a)}\cap R_{u(b)})?

We remark that we are only able to provide bounds for
H(R_{u(a)}|R_{u(b)})
at present. To tackle Problem
6
, it seems to us that probably a vast generalization of the Ocneanu’s compactness theorem beyond symmetric commuting squares is required, which is out of reach at present with the help of existing tools in the literature.

For a general pair of
n\times n
complex Hadamard matrices, we are not yet able to compute these invariants. Our work in this article leads to many interesting open problems that should lay the foundation for developing a general theory of two subfactors and non-commutaive entropy à la Connes-Størmer. We hope to tackle some of these problems in years to come.

9
Appendix

We provide a proof of the Jones’ basic construction for the unital inclusion
\mathbb{C}\subset u\Delta_{n}u^{*}
for
n=2\mbox{ and }4
. Although tower of spin model basic construction is well-known in the literature, our tower is slightly different than the one in
[
29
]
as it is more convenient for our purpose. The Jones’ projection for the inclusion
\mathbb{C}\subset\Delta_{n}
will be denoted by
J_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i,j}E_{ij}\in M_{n}
, and that for the inclusion
\Delta_{n}\subset M_{n}
will be denoted by
e_{1}=\mbox{diag}\{E_{11},\ldots,E_{nn}\}\in\Delta_{n}\otimes M_{n}
.

9.1
Basic construction for the
2\times 2
case

Let
u=(u_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}
be an arbitrary complex Hadamard matrix in
M_{2}
and consider the following matrices

\eta=\left[{\begin{matrix}\frac{u_{12}}{u_{11}}&0\\
0&\frac{u_{22}}{u_{21}}\\
\end{matrix}}\right]\quad\mbox{and}\quad\xi=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\,\left[{\begin{matrix}1&\frac{u_{12}}{u_{11}}\\
1&\frac{u_{22}}{u_{21}}\\
\end{matrix}}\right]\,,

associated with
u
. Since
u
is unitary, it is easy to check that
\,\eta\mbox{ and }\xi
are unitary matrices.

Lemma 9.1
.

Consider the Jones’ projection
e_{1}=\mathrm{diag}\{E_{11},E_{22}\}\in\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}
for the inclusion
\,\Delta_{2}\subset M_{2}
. The
\,4\times 4
unitary matrix
\,u_{1}\,
in
\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}
satisfying
(u\Delta_{2}u^{*})\,e_{1}\,(u\Delta_{2}u^{*})=u_{1}M_{2}u_{1}^{*}
is given by
u_{1}=\mbox{bl-diag}\{u,u\eta\}
.

Proof
:
Observe that
\Delta_{2}u^{*}\,e_{1}\,u\Delta_{2}=\mbox{bl-diag}\{\Delta_{2}u^{*}E_{11}u\Delta_{2}\,,\,\Delta_{2}u^{*}E_{22}u\Delta_{2}\}
. Using the fact that
u
is a complex Hadamard matrix, i,e. all the entries of
u
are of same modulus, and
\overline{u_{21}}u_{22}=\displaystyle{\frac{\overline{u_{11}u_{22}}\,u_{12}^{2}}{\overline{u_{21}}\,u_{11}}}
because
u_{11}\overline{u_{21}}+u_{12}\overline{u_{22}}=0
, it is easy to check that
\eta u^{*}E_{11}u\eta^{*}=u^{*}E_{22}u\,
. Hence,
\Delta_{2}u^{*}E_{22}u\Delta_{2}=\eta\big(\Delta_{2}u^{*}E_{11}u\Delta_{2}\big)\eta^{*}
. This gives us the following,

(I_{2}\otimes u)(I_{2}\otimes\Delta_{2})(I_{2}\otimes u^{*})\,\mbox{bl-diag}\{E_{11},E_{22}\}\,(I_{2}\otimes u)(I_{2}\otimes\Delta_{2})(I_{2}\otimes u^{*})=u_{1}(I_{2}\otimes M_{2})u_{1}^{*}

with
u_{1}
as the required matrix if we show that
\mbox{Alg}\,\big\{\Delta_{2}u^{*}E_{11}u\Delta_{2}\big\}=M_{2}\,
. However, this is obvious as
E_{ii}u^{*}E_{11}uE_{jj}
gives the matrix unit
E_{ij}
in
M_{2}
.

\Box

Lemma 9.2
.

Consider the Jones’ projection
e_{2}=J_{2}\otimes I_{2}\in M_{2}\otimes M_{2}
for the inclusion
\mathbb{C}\otimes M_{2}\subset\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}
. The
4\times 4
unitary matrix
\,u_{2}\,
in
M_{2}\otimes M_{2}
satisfying
(u_{1}M_{2}u_{1}^{*})\,e_{2}\,(u_{1}M_{2}u_{1}^{*})=u_{2}(\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2})u_{2}^{*}
is given by
\,u_{2}=u_{1}(\xi^{*}\otimes I_{2})
.

Proof
:
First observe that

u_{1}^{*}(J_{2}\otimes I_{2})u_{1}=\left[{\begin{matrix}I_{2}&\eta\\
\eta^{*}&I_{2}\\
\end{matrix}}\right]\,.

Then, using the fact that
\xi
is unitary we have the following,

\displaystyle u_{1}(I_{2}\otimes M_{2})u_{1}^{*}(J_{2}\otimes I_{2})u_{1}(I_{2}\otimes M_{2})u_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{bl-diag}\{u,u\eta\}(\xi^{*}\otimes I_{2})(I_{2}\otimes A)(\xi\otimes I_{2})\left[{\begin{matrix}I_{2}&\eta\\
\eta^{*}&I_{2}\\
\end{matrix}}\right](\xi^{*}\otimes I_{2})(I_{2}\otimes B)(\xi\otimes I_{2})\,\mbox{bl-diag}\{u^{*},\eta^{*}u^{*}\}

where
A,B\in M_{2}(\mathbb{C})
. Now, it is easy to see that
I_{2}+\frac{u_{22}}{u_{21}}\eta^{*}+\displaystyle{\frac{\overline{u_{12}}}{\overline{u_{11}}}}\eta+\frac{u_{22}}{u_{21}}\displaystyle{\frac{\overline{u_{12}}}{\overline{u_{11}}}}I_{2}=0
which gives us the following,

\displaystyle(\xi\otimes I_{2})\left[{\begin{matrix}I_{2}&\eta\\
\eta^{*}&I_{2}\\
\end{matrix}}\right](\xi^{*}\otimes I_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\,\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{2I_{2}+\frac{u_{12}}{u_{11}}\eta^{*}+\frac{\overline{u_{12}}}{\overline{u_{11}}}\eta\,,\,2I_{2}+\frac{u_{22}}{u_{21}}\eta^{*}+\frac{\overline{u_{22}}}{\overline{u_{21}}}\eta\right\}\,.

Since
\frac{1}{4}\big(2I_{2}+\frac{u_{12}}{u_{11}}\eta^{*}+\displaystyle{\frac{\overline{u_{12}}}{\overline{u_{11}}}}\eta\big)=E_{11}
and
\frac{1}{4}\big(2I_{2}+\frac{u_{22}}{u_{21}}\eta^{*}+\displaystyle{\frac{\overline{u_{22}}}{\overline{u_{21}}}}\eta\big)=E_{22}
, and we have

\mbox{Alg}\left\{\mbox{bl-diag}\{AE_{11}B,AE_{22}B\}\,:\,A,B\in M_{2}\right\}=\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}\,,

the proof is concluded.

\Box

Proof of Theorem
6.1
:
We prove by strong induction on
k
, where Lemma
9.1
and
9.2
are the basis step for part
(ii)
and
(i)
respectively. The Jones’ projection for the inclusion
\mathbb{C}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\subset\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}
is
e_{2k}=J_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)}\in M_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}
, and that for
\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\subset M_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}
is
e_{2k+1}=\mbox{diag}\{E_{11},E_{22}\}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)}\in\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}
. First we prove part
(i)
. Assume that the result is true up to
u_{2k-1}
for some
k\geq 2
, and consider the Fourier matrix
F_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left[{\begin{smallmatrix}1&1\\
1&-1\\
\end{smallmatrix}}\right]
. Then, we have the following,

\displaystyle u_{2k-1}\left(I_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)u_{2k-1}^{*}e_{2k}u_{2k-1}\left(I_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)u_{2k-1}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{2k-1}\big(I_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\big)\,\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{I_{2}^{(k)},\eta^{(k-1)}\right\}\left(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k-2}^{*}\right)\left(J_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)}\right)\left(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k-2}\right)

\displaystyle\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{I_{2}^{(k)},\eta^{(k-1)}\right\}\left(I_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)u_{2k-1}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{2k-1}\left(I_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{I_{2}^{(k)},\eta^{(k-1)}\right\}\left(J_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)}\right)\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{I_{2}^{(k)},\eta^{(k-1)}\right\}\left(I_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)u_{2k-1}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{2k-1}\left(I_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)\left(\mbox{diag}\{1,1,1,-1\}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)\left(J_{2}\otimes I_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)\left(\mbox{diag}\{1,1,1,-1\}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)

\displaystyle\left(I_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)u_{2k-1}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{2k-1}\left(I_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)\left(F_{2}\otimes I_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}(I_{2}\otimes I_{2}+F_{2}\sigma_{1}F_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3})\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)\left(F_{2}\otimes I_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)

\displaystyle\left(I_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)u_{2k-1}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{2k-1}\left(F_{2}\otimes I_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)\left(\left(F_{2}\otimes I_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)\left(I_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)\left(F_{2}\otimes I_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2}(I_{2}\otimes I_{2}+F_{2}\sigma_{1}F_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3})\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)\left(\left(F_{2}\otimes I_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)\left(I_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)\left(F_{2}\otimes I_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\left(F_{2}\otimes I_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)u_{2k-1}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{2k-1}\left(\xi_{k}\otimes I_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)\left((F_{2}\otimes I_{2})(I_{2}\otimes M_{2})(F_{2}\otimes I_{2})\otimes M_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)\left(\mbox{bl-diag}\{E_{11},E_{22}\}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)

\displaystyle\left((F_{2}\otimes I_{2})(I_{2}\otimes M_{2})(F_{2}\otimes I_{2})\otimes M_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)\left(\xi_{k}\otimes I_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)^{*}u_{2k-1}^{*}\,.

From here, part
(i)
will follow if the following holds

\displaystyle\mbox{Alg}\,\big\{\mbox{Ad}_{F_{2}\otimes I_{2}}(I_{2}\otimes A)\,\mbox{bl-diag}\{E_{11},E_{22}\}\,\mbox{Ad}_{F_{2}\otimes I_{2}}(I_{2}\otimes B)\,:\,A,B\in M_{2}\big\}=\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}\,.\qquad

(9.34)

Now, the equality in
Equation

9.34
holds because
\mbox{Ad}_{F_{2}\otimes I_{2}}(I_{2}\otimes A)=I_{2}\otimes A
, and

\mbox{Alg}\left\{\mbox{bl-diag}\{AE_{11}B,AE_{22}B\}:A,B\in M_{2}\right\}=\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}

as in Lemma
9.2
, which completes part
(i)
.

Now, we prove part
(ii)
. Assume that the result is true up to
u_{2k-1}
for some
k\geq 2
. We also have
u_{2k}
in our hand, since part
(i)
is already proved. First observe that

\displaystyle(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k}^{*})e_{2k+1}(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{u_{2k}^{*}(E_{11}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)})u_{2k}\,,\,u_{2k}^{*}(E_{22}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)})u_{2k}\right\}\,.

We claim that for
\eta_{k}=\mbox{diag}\{1,-1\}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)}\,,
one has the following,

\displaystyle u_{2k}^{*}\left(E_{22}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)}\right)u_{2k}=\eta_{k}\left(u_{2k}^{*}(E_{11}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)})u_{2k}\right)\eta_{k}\,.

(9.35)

To prove this, we first write
u_{2k}=u_{2k-1}\big(\xi_{k}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)}\big)
using part
(i)
, and then using the induction hypothesis we get that

\displaystyle u_{2k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k-2}\right)\left((E_{11}+E_{12})\otimes I_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}+(E_{21}-E_{22})\otimes\sigma_{3}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\right)\,.

Now, it is a straightforward verification that Eqn.
9.35
holds. Therefore, we get the following,

\displaystyle(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k})\left(I_{2}\otimes\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k})^{*}e_{2k+1}(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k})\left(I_{2}\otimes\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k})^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k})\left(I_{2}\otimes\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)\,\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{I_{2}^{(k+1)},\eta_{k}\right\}\left(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k}^{*}(E_{11}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)})u_{2k}\right)

\displaystyle\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{I_{2}^{(k+1)},\eta_{k}\right\}\left(I_{2}\otimes\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k})^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k})\left(E_{11}\otimes I_{2}^{(k+1)}+E_{22}\otimes\eta_{k}\right)\left(I_{2}\otimes\left(\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)u_{2k}^{*}\left(E_{11}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)}\right)u_{2k}\left(\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\left(E_{11}\otimes I_{2}^{(k+1)}+E_{22}\otimes\eta_{k}\right)(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k})^{*}\,.

This is equal to
\mbox{Ad}_{u_{2k+1}}(I_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k+1)})
, where
\,u_{2k+1}=\left(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k}\right)\left(E_{11}\otimes I_{2}^{(k+1)}+E_{22}\otimes\eta_{k}\right)
, if

\mbox{Alg}\,\left\{\left(\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)u_{2k}^{*}\left(E_{11}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)}\right)u_{2k}\left(\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\right)\right\}=M_{2}\otimes M_{2}^{(k)}\,.

We claim that
\,u_{2k}^{*}\left(E_{11}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)}\right)u_{2k}=J_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)}
. To see this, observe that

\displaystyle u_{2k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{2k-1}\left(F_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(I_{2}\otimes u_{2k-2}\right)\big(\left((E_{11}+E_{12})\otimes I_{2}+(E_{21}-E_{22})\otimes\sigma_{3}\right)\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}\big)\,,

and hence

u_{2k}^{*}\left(E_{11}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)}\right)u_{2k}=J_{2}\otimes I_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k-1)}=J_{2}\otimes I_{2}^{(k)}\,.

Since
\Delta_{2}J_{2}\Delta_{2}=M_{2}
, proof of part
(ii)
is now completed.

\Box

9.2
Basic construction for the
4\times 4
case

Consider the
4\times 4
complex Hadamard matrix

u=\frac{1}{2}\,\left[{\begin{matrix}1&1&1&1\\
1&ia&-1&-ia\\
1&-1&1&-1\\
1&-ia&-1&ia\\
\end{matrix}}\right]

where
a\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
(semicircle to be precise).

Lemma 9.3
.

Let
e_{1}=\mbox{diag}\{E_{11},E_{22},E_{33},E_{44}\}\in\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}
, which is the Jones’ projection for the basic construction of
\,\Delta_{4}\subset M_{4}
. The
16\times 16
unitary matrix
\,u_{1}
in
\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}
such that
(u\Delta_{4}u^{*})\,e_{1}\,(u\Delta_{4}u^{*})=u_{1}M_{4}u_{1}^{*}
is given by the block-diagonal matrix bl-diag
\big\{u,u\,W_{1}^{(a)},u\,W_{2}^{(a)},u\,W_{3}^{(a)}\big\}
.

Proof
:
It is easy to verify the following,

\displaystyle u^{*}E_{22}u

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sigma_{3}\otimes\mbox{diag}\{i,\overline{a}\}\right)u^{*}E_{11}u\left(\sigma_{3}\otimes\mbox{diag}\{-i,a\}\right)=W_{1}^{(a)}u^{*}E_{11}uW_{3}^{(\overline{a})}\,,

\displaystyle u^{*}E_{33}u

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(I_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\right)u^{*}E_{11}u\left(I_{2}\otimes\sigma_{3}\right)=W_{2}^{(a)}u^{*}E_{11}uW_{2}^{(a)}\,,

\displaystyle u^{*}E_{44}u

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sigma_{3}\otimes\mbox{diag}\{-i,\overline{a}\}\right)u^{*}E_{11}u\left(\sigma_{3}\otimes\mbox{diag}\{i,a\}\right)=W_{3}^{(a)}u^{*}E_{11}uW_{1}^{(\overline{a})}\,.

Therefore,

\displaystyle u\Delta_{4}u^{*}e_{1}u\Delta_{4}u^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I_{4}\otimes u)\,\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{I_{4},W_{1}^{(a)},W_{2}^{(a)},W_{3}^{(a)}\right\}(I_{4}\otimes\Delta_{4}u^{*}E_{11}u\Delta_{4})(I_{4}\otimes u^{*})\,\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{I_{4},W_{3}^{(\overline{a})},W_{2}^{(a)},W_{1}^{(\overline{a})}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{1}(I_{4}\otimes M_{4})u_{1}^{*}

with
u_{1}
in the required form if
\mbox{Alg}\,\big\{\Delta_{4}u^{*}E_{11}u\Delta_{4}\big\}=M_{4}
, i,e.,
\mbox{Alg}\,\big\{\Delta_{4}(J_{2}\otimes J_{2})\Delta_{4}\big\}=M_{4}
. But since
J_{4}
is the Jones’ projection for
\mathbb{C}\subseteq\Delta_{4}\subseteq M_{4}
, this is obvious.

\Box

Lemma 9.4
.

Let
e_{2}=J_{4}\otimes I_{4}\in M_{4}\otimes M_{4}
, which is the Jones’ projection for the basic construction of
\mathbb{C}\otimes M_{4}\subset\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}
. The
16\times 16
unitary matrix
\,u_{2}\,
in
M_{4}\otimes M_{4}
, where
(u_{1}M_{4}u_{1}^{*})\,e_{2}\,(u_{1}M_{4}u_{1}^{*})=u_{2}(\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4})u_{2}^{*}\,,
is given by
\,u_{2}=u_{1}(\xi\otimes I_{4})
.

Proof
:
First observe that

u_{1}^{*}e_{2}u_{1}=\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{u^{*},\left(W_{1}^{(a)}\right)^{*}u^{*},W_{2}^{(a)}u^{*},\left(W_{3}^{(a)}\right)^{*}u^{*}\right\}(J_{4}\otimes I_{4})\,\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{u,uW_{1}^{(a)},uW_{2}^{(a)},uW_{3}^{(a)}\right\}.

and
(\xi\otimes I_{4})^{*}u_{1}^{*}e_{2}u_{1}(\xi\otimes I_{4})=16e_{1}\,,
i,e. the unitary
\xi\otimes I_{4}
diagonalize
u_{1}^{*}e_{2}u_{1}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle u_{1}M_{4}u_{1}^{*}e_{2}u_{1}M_{4}u_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{1}\left((I_{4}\otimes M_{4})(\xi\otimes I_{4})\right)\left((\xi\otimes I_{4})^{*}u_{1}^{*}e_{2}u_{1}(\xi\otimes I_{4})\right)\left((\xi\otimes I_{4})^{*}(I_{4}\otimes M_{4})\right)u_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{1}(\xi\otimes I_{4})\left((I_{4}\otimes M_{4})e_{1}(I_{4}\otimes M_{4})\right)(\xi\otimes I_{4})^{*}u_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{2}\left(\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}\right)u_{2}^{*}

with
u_{2}=u_{1}(\xi\otimes I_{4})
, if we can show that
\mbox{Alg}\{(I_{4}\otimes M_{4})e_{1}(I_{4}\otimes M_{4})\}=\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}
. To conclude the following,

\mbox{Alg}\big\{\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{AE_{11}B,AE_{22}B,AE_{33}B,AE_{44}B\right\}\,:\,A,B\in M_{4}\big\}=\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}

observe that taking
A=E_{ik}
and
B=E_{kj}
we obtain the matrix
E_{kk}\otimes E_{ij}
in
\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}
for
k,i,j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
.

\Box

Proof of Theorem
7.1
:

We prove by strong induction on
k
. Lemma
9.3
and
9.4
are the basis step for part
(i)
and
(ii)
respectively. The Jones’ projection for the basic construction of
\mathbb{C}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)}\subset\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)}
is
e_{2k}=J_{4}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}\in M_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)}
, and that for
\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)}\subset M_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)}
is
e_{1}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}\in\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)}
.

First we prove part
(i)
. Assume that the result is true up to
u_{2k}
. Observe that
e_{2k+1}=e_{1}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}
. Now, we have the following,

\displaystyle\left(I_{4}\otimes(\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)})\right)(I_{4}\otimes u_{2k})^{*}(e_{1}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)})(I_{4}\otimes u_{2k})\left(I_{4}\otimes(\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(I_{4}\otimes(\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)})\right)\left(I_{4}\otimes(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k)})^{*}u_{2k-1}^{*}\right)\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{4}E_{jj}\otimes(E_{jj}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)})\Big)

\displaystyle\left(I_{4}\otimes u_{2k-1}(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k)})\right)\left(I_{4}\otimes(\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{4}E_{jj}\otimes\left((\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)})(\xi^{*}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)})u_{2k-1}^{*}(E_{jj}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)})u_{2k-1}(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k)})(\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{4}E_{jj}\otimes\left((\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)})(\xi^{*}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)})(E_{jj}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)})(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k)})(\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{4}\left(E_{jj}\otimes\Delta_{4}\xi^{*}E_{jj}\xi\Delta_{4}\right)\otimes M_{4}^{(k)}\,.

Here, the second last equality follows from the following fact that

u_{2k-1}^{*}\left(E_{jj}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}\right)u_{2k-1}=E_{jj}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}

by using the induction hypothesis and the fact that
W_{j}^{(a)},\,j=1,2,3,
are unitary matrices. Now, it is a straightforward verification that

\xi^{*}E_{22}\xi=W_{1}^{(a)}\xi^{*}E_{11}\xi\big(W_{1}^{(a)}\big)^{*}\,,\,\xi^{*}E_{33}\xi=W_{2}^{(a)}\xi^{*}E_{11}\xi\big(W_{2}^{(a)}\big)^{*}\,,\,\xi^{*}E_{44}\xi=W_{3}^{(a)}\xi^{*}E_{11}\xi\big(W_{3}^{(a)}\big)^{*}\,.

Therefore, if we set
W_{0}^{(a)}=I_{4}
, then we have the following,

\displaystyle(I_{4}\otimes u_{2k})\big(I_{4}\otimes(\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)})\big)(I_{4}\otimes u_{2k})^{*}(e_{1}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)})(I_{4}\otimes u_{2k})\big(I_{4}\otimes(\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)})\big)(I_{4}\otimes u_{2k})^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(I_{4}\otimes u_{2k})\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{4}\left((I_{4}\otimes W_{j}^{(a)})(E_{jj}\otimes\Delta_{4}\xi^{*}E_{11}\xi\Delta_{4})(I_{4}\otimes W_{3}^{(a)})^{*}\right)\otimes M_{4}^{(k)}\Big)(I_{4}\otimes u_{2k})^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{u_{2k},u_{2k}\left(W_{1}^{(a)}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}\right),u_{2k}\left(W_{2}^{(a)}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}\right),u_{2k}\left(W_{3}^{(a)}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}\right)\right\}\big((I_{4}\otimes\Delta_{4}\xi^{*}E_{11}\xi\Delta_{4})\otimes M_{4}^{(k)}\big)

\displaystyle\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{u_{2k}^{*},\left(W_{1}^{(a)}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}\right)^{*}u_{2k}^{*},\left(W_{2}^{(a)}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}\right)u_{2k}^{*},\left(W_{3}^{(a)}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}\right)^{*}u_{2k}^{*}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{2k+1}\big(I_{4}\otimes M_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)}\big)u_{2k+1}^{*}

with
u_{2k+1}
in the required form if we show that
\Delta_{4}\big(\xi^{*}E_{11}\xi\big)\Delta_{4}=M_{4}\,.
However, this is obvious since
\xi^{*}E_{11}\xi=J_{4}
, which is a direct verification, and
\Delta_{4}J_{4}\Delta_{4}=M_{4}
because for any
i,j\in\{1,\ldots,4\}
, one has
E_{ii}J_{4}E_{jj}=E_{ij}
.

Now we prove part
(ii)
. Assume that the result is true up to
u_{2k}
. We also have
u_{2k+1}
in our hand now since part (i) is already proved. Observe that
e_{2k+2}=J_{4}\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}
. Now, we have the following,

\displaystyle\left(I_{4}\otimes(M_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)})\right)u_{2k+1}^{*}\left(J_{4}\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)u_{2k+1}\left(I_{4}\otimes(M_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)\left((\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)})^{*}(I_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k+1)})(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)})\right)\Big((\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)})^{*}u_{2k+1}^{*}(J_{4}\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)})

\displaystyle u_{2k+1}(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)})\Big)\left((\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)})^{*}(I_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k+1)})(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)})\right)\left(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)})\right)^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)(I_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k+1)})\left((\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)})^{*}u_{2k+1}^{*}(J_{4}\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)})u_{2k+1}(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)})\right)

\displaystyle(I_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k+1)})\left(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)^{*}\,.

Now, using part (i) for
u_{2k+1}
and the induction hypothesis, we have the following,

\displaystyle\left(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)^{*}u_{2k+1}^{*}\left(J_{4}\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)u_{2k+1}\left(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)^{*}\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{I_{4}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)},\left(W_{1}^{(a)}\right)^{*}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)},W_{2}^{(a)}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)},\left(W_{3}^{(a)}\right)^{*}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}\right\}(I_{4}\otimes u_{2k})^{*}

\displaystyle\left(J_{4}\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)(I_{4}\otimes u_{2k})\,\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{I_{4}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)},W_{1}^{(a)}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)},W_{2}^{(a)}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)},W_{3}^{(a)}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}\right\}\left(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)^{*}\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{\xi^{*}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)},\left(W_{1}^{(a)}\right)^{*}\xi^{*}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)},W_{2}^{(a)}\xi^{*}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)},\left(W_{3}^{(a)}\right)^{*}\xi^{*}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}\right\}

\displaystyle\left(J_{4}\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)\,\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k)},\xi W_{1}^{(a)}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)},\xi W_{2}^{(a)}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)},\xi W_{3}^{(a)}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}\right\}\left(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)^{*}\Big(\Big(\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{\xi^{*},\left(W_{1}^{(a)}\right)^{*}\xi^{*},W_{2}^{(a)}\xi^{*},\left(W_{3}^{(a)}\right)^{*}\xi^{*}\right\}(J_{4}\otimes I_{4})

\displaystyle\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{\xi,\xi W_{1}^{(a)},\xi W_{2}^{(a)},\xi W_{3}^{(a)}\right\}\Big)\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}\Big)\left(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)^{*}\Big(\Big(\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{I_{4},\left(W_{1}^{(a)}\right)^{*},W_{2}^{(a)},\left(W_{3}^{(a)}\right)^{*}\right\}(J_{4}\otimes I_{4})

\displaystyle\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{I_{4},W_{1}^{(a)},W_{2}^{(a)},W_{3}^{(a)}\right\}\Big)\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}\Big)\left(\xi\otimes I_{4}^{(k+1)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big((\xi^{*}\otimes I_{4})\,\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{I_{4},\left(W_{1}^{(a)}\right)^{*},W_{2}^{(a)},\left(W_{3}^{(a)}\right)^{*}\right\}(J_{4}\otimes I_{4})

\displaystyle\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{I_{4},W_{1}^{(a)},W_{2}^{(a)},W_{3}^{(a)}\right\}(\xi\otimes I_{4})\Big)\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{E_{11},E_{22},E_{33},E_{44}\right\}\otimes I_{4}^{(k)}

in
\Delta_{4}\otimes\Delta_{4}\otimes M_{4}^{(k)}
. Here, the last line follows from the proof of Lemma
9.4
. We finally observe that

\mbox{Alg}\left\{(I_{4}\otimes A)\mbox{bl-diag}\left\{E_{11},E_{22},E_{33},E_{44}\right\}(I_{4}\otimes B):A,B\in M_{4}(\mathbb{C})\right\}=\Delta_{2}\otimes M_{4}(\mathbb{C})

which concludes the proof of part (ii).

\Box
